Even when I'm exhausted from cleaning buildings all day, I lie awake for hours staring at the ceiling and wake up constantly worrying, leaving me with so little energy that I just do the bare minimum and go home to lie around.\n\nDoctor: Have you also noticed a significant loss of interest or pleasure in activities you used to enjoy, like hobbies or spending time with others?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. My structured hobbies, like neighborhood clean-ups and financial planning, just don't feel meaningful to me anymore. I've also been turning down or canceling plans with friends and neighbors so much that they've pretty much stopped asking me to help or hang out.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself struggling with intense feelings of worthlessness, or blaming yourself excessively for things that go wrong?"
  }

It has pretty much become my baseline, and most days it just feels like there's a gray cloud hanging over everything.\n\nDoctor: During these past three years, have you ever had a period of two months or more where you felt completely back to your normal self?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any breaks like that at all. This heavy, discouraged feeling has been my constant baseline, day in and day out, ever since I got passed over for that promotion three years ago. I haven't felt back to my normal self for even a few weeks, let alone two months.\n\nDoctor: During these past three years of feeling down, have you also noticed any persistent changes in your sleep, appetite, or energy levels?\n\nPatient: My sleep has been terrible and my energy is very low, though I haven't noticed much change in my appetite. Even when I'm exhausted from cleaning buildings all day, I lie awake for hours staring at the ceiling and wake up constantly worrying, leaving me with so little energy that I just do the bare minimum and go home to lie around.\n\nDoctor: Have you also noticed a significant loss of interest or pleasure in activities you used to enjoy, like hobbies or spending time with others?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. My structured hobbies, like neighborhood clean-ups and financial planning, just don't feel meaningful to me anymore. I've also been turning down or canceling plans with friends and neighbors so much that they've pretty much stopped asking me to help or hang out.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself struggling with intense feelings of worthlessness, or blaming yourself excessively for things that go wrong?\n\nPatient: Yes, I constantly feel like I'm just not the kind of person who is meant to succeed or move up in life. Getting passed over for that supervisor job three years ago really confirmed that for me. It made me feel like no matter how much I study, plan, or volunteer, I'm simply not good or smart enough to get ahead.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of harming yourself, or felt that life is no longer worth living?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any thoughts of hurting myself or anything like that. But I do often feel really empty and hopeless inside, like I'm just stuck in this rut and nothing is ever going to change for me.\n\nDoctor: During these past three years, has this low mood stayed at a steady, mild-to-moderate level, or have there been periods where it became so severe that you found it nearly impossible to work or manage daily tasks?\n\nPatient: It has been a steady, constant baseline like a gray cloud over me for the last three years, rather than coming in severe, paralyzing waves. While it hasn't been completely impossible to work, I've scaled back to doing just the bare minimum at my job and have stopped participating in social activities and extra projects.\n\nDoctor: Have you also found it more difficult to concentrate, focus on your tasks, or make decisions over these past three years?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes, concentration has become really hard lately; I'll catch myself reading the same email from my supervisor three or four times, or just standing in the supply closet blanking on what I even went in there for. Even simple decisions, like trying to choose which certification class to sign up for, feels like my brain is completely stuck in mud.

While it hasn't been completely impossible to work, I've scaled back to doing just the bare minimum at my job and have stopped participating in social activities and extra projects.\n\nDoctor: Have you also found it more difficult to concentrate, focus on your tasks, or make decisions over these past three years?\n\nPatient: Yes, concentration has become really hard lately; I'll catch myself reading the same email from my supervisor three or four times, or just standing in the supply closet blanking on what I even went in there for. Even simple decisions, like trying to choose which certification class to sign up for, feels like my brain is completely stuck in mud.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had periods lasting a few days or more where you felt the exact opposite—unusually happy, hyperactive, or full of energy, with much less need for sleep?\n\nPatient: No, I’ve never had any times where I felt unusually happy, hyperactive, or needed less sleep. My mood has pretty much just been consistently low and flat this whole time."

While it hasn't been completely impossible to work, I've scaled back to doing just the bare minimum at my job and have stopped participating in social activities and extra projects.\n\nDoctor: Have you also found it more difficult to concentrate, focus on your tasks, or make decisions over these past three years?\n\nPatient: Yes, concentration has become really hard lately; I'll catch myself reading the same email from my supervisor three or four times, or just standing in the supply closet blanking on what I even went in there for. My mood has pretty much just been consistently low and flat this whole time.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any medical conditions, like thyroid issues, or do you regularly use alcohol, recreational drugs, or any medications?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have any medical conditions like thyroid issues, and I'm not taking any medications right now.
